## GHSA-fmc8-p6q7-75cc · github.com/canonical/lxd

2026-11-06 · Go · **CRITICAL** 9.9 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from insufficient AppArmor confinement when handling user-supplied compression algorithms. The AppArmor profile allowed the compression command to write to any path specified by the user, and the command arguments themselves were not properly sanitized, leading to argument injection.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary file write (AFW) and potentially arbitrary code execution (ACE) by injecting arguments into the compression command or by directing the output to sensitive file paths.

### Key diff

```
Before:
```go
// No specific AppArmor profile for user-supplied compression commands.
// Command arguments were not explicitly sanitized or restricted.
```
After:
```go
// CompressWrapper should be used when applying a user specified compression algorithm.
// The given command is directly modified such that it runs under `aa-exec` with a restricted profile (see [compressProfileTpl]).
// It returns a cleanup function that deletes the AppArmor profile that the command is running in.
func CompressWrapper(sysOS *sys.OS, cmd *exec.Cmd, dstPath string, extraAllowedCommands []string) (func(), error) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new AppArmor profile specifically for user-supplied compression algorithms. This profile strictly limits write access to only the designated output file and explicitly lists allowed command paths, preventing arbitrary file writes and argument injection.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 128 occurrences**

## GHSA-9fpx-fr6j-2vh6 · GeoNode

2026-11-06 · Python · **HIGH** 8.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The application constructed shell commands by directly concatenating unsanitized user-controlled input (original_name, alternate, input_file) into a string that was then executed via Popen with shell=True. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary shell commands.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary operating system commands on the server, potentially leading to full system compromise, data exfiltration, or denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/geonode/upload/handlers/common/vector.py
+++ b/geonode/upload/handlers/common/vector.py
-        options += f'"{input_file}"' + f" -lco FID={DEFAULT_PK_COLUMN_NAME} "
-
-        options += f'-nln {alternate} "{original_name}"'
+        options += f" {shlex.quote(input_file)} -lco FID={DEFAULT_PK_COLUMN_NAME} "
+
+        options += f" -nln {shlex.quote(alternate)} {shlex.quote(original_name)}"
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces `shlex.quote()` to properly escape user-controlled inputs before they are included in the command string. Additionally, it refactors the command execution to use `shell=False` with a list of arguments, which prevents shell interpretation of the arguments, and handles piped commands explicitly in Python.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 128 occurrences**

## GHSA-j93m-3j9p-m5m8 · github.com/canonical/lxd

2026-11-06 · Go · **MODERATE** 6.5 · Pattern: `NULL_DEREF→CRASH`

### Root cause

The application processed backup configurations that could contain nil entries within snapshot lists. When iterating over these lists, the code did not check if individual snapshot entries were nil before attempting to access their properties (e.g., .Name, .ExpiresAt), leading to a nil-pointer dereference.

### Impact

An attacker providing a specially crafted backup configuration with nil snapshot entries could trigger a nil-pointer dereference, causing the LXD daemon to crash and resulting in a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lxd/storage/backend_lxd.go
+++ b/lxd/storage/backend_lxd.go
@@ -840,7 +840,11 @@ func (b *lxdBackend) CreateInstanceFromBackup(srcBackup backup.Info, srcData io.
 		}
 	}
 
-	for _, snap := range srcBackup.Config.Snapshots {
+	for i, snap := range srcBackup.Config.Snapshots {
+		if snap == nil {
+			return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf("Nil instance snapshot definition found at index %d", i)
+		}
+
 		snapInstName := srcBackup.Name + shared.SnapshotDelimiter + snap.Name
 		err = instancetype.ValidName(snapInstName, true)
 		if err != nil
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds explicit nil checks for each snapshot entry within loops that iterate over snapshot lists in backup configurations. If a nil snapshot is encountered, an error is returned, preventing the nil-pointer dereference.

## GHSA-r4hm-p9f9-fj9v · GeoNode

2026-11-06 · Python · **MODERATE** 6.3 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The application did not properly sanitize user-supplied input in the 'abstract' field of document metadata before storing and later rendering it. This allowed attackers to inject malicious HTML or script tags.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary web scripts into the application, which would execute in the context of a user's browser when they view the affected metadata. This could lead to session hijacking, defacement, or redirection to malicious sites.

### Key diff

```
--- /dev/null
+++ b/geonode/metadata/handlers/meta.py
@@ -0,0 +1,102 @@
+    def pre_deserialization(self, resource, jsonschema: dict, instance: dict, partial: set, context: dict):
+        errors = context["errors"]
+        self._sanitize_instance(instance, context, errors)
```

### Fix summary

A new `CleanupHandler` was introduced to sanitize metadata fields before deserialization. It uses `BeautifulSoup` to parse HTML-like strings, removes dangerous tags (like script, style, iframe), and then extracts plain text, preventing the injection of malicious code.

---

## GHSA-fqpv-mq26-pr4j · GeoNode

2026-11-06 · Python · **MODERATE** 5.5 · Pattern: `UNCLASSIFIED`

### Root cause

The application previously allowed users to upload files based solely on their file extension, without verifying the actual content type of the file. This meant an attacker could rename a malicious script (e.g., a PHP or JSP file) to have a seemingly harmless extension (e.g., .jpg or .txt) and upload it, bypassing basic checks.

### Impact

An attacker could upload arbitrary malicious files, potentially leading to remote code execution on the server, defacement of the website, or other severe system compromises, depending on the server's configuration and the type of file uploaded.

### Key diff

```
--- a/geonode/upload/uploadhandler.py
+++ b/geonode/upload/uploadhandler.py
@@ -19,17 +19,132 @@
 import base64
 import binascii
 import html
+from pathlib import Path
 
+import magic
 from django.conf import settings
 from django.core.exceptions import RequestDataTooBig, TooManyFieldsSent
 from django.core.files.uploadedfile import SimpleUploadedFile
-from django.core.files.uploadhandler import FileUploadHandler
+from django.core.files.uploadhandler import FileUploadHandler, StopUpload
 from django.http import QueryDict
 from django.http.multipartparser import FIELD, FILE, ChunkIter, LazyStream, Parser, exhaust
 from django.utils.datastructures import MultiValueDict
 from django.utils.encoding import force_str
 from django.urls import reverse
+
+class FileValidationUploadHandler(FileUploadHandler):
+    sniff_bytes = 8192
+
+    def handle_raw_input(self, input_data, META, content_length, boundary, encoding=None):
+        # ... (initialization and config loading)
+
+    def new_file(self, *args, **kwargs):
+        # ... (extension check and config setup)
+
+    def receive_data_chunk(self, raw_data, start):
+        # ... (buffer data and trigger validation)
+
+    def file_complete(self, file_size):
+        # ... (validate small files)
+
+    def _validate_magic_mime(self):
+        sample = bytes(self._buffer)
+
+        if self.extension in self.magic_mimetype_map:
+            self.detected_mime = self._detect_mime(sample)
+            if self.detected_mime not in self.magic_mimetype_map[self.extension]:
+                self._reject(
+                    f"Detected content type '{self.detected_mime}' does not match extension '.{self.extension}'."
+                )
+
+        if self.extension in self.magic_description_map:
+            self.detected_description = self._detect_description(sample)
+            expected_substrings = self.magic_description_map[self.extension]
+            if not any(token in self.detected_description for token in expected_substrings):
+                self._reject(f"File content does not match expected signature for '.{self.extension}'.")
+
+        self._checked = True
+
+    def _reject(self, reason):
+        self.request.upload_validation_error = reason
+        raise StopUpload(connection_reset=False)
+
+    def _detect_mime(self, sample):
+        return magic.from_buffer(sample, mime=True)
+
+    def _detect_description(self, sample):
+        return magic.from_buffer(sample)
 
 
 class SizeRestrictedFileUploadHandler(FileUploadHandler):
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `FileValidationUploadHandler` that performs content-type validation using `libmagic`. It sniffs the initial bytes of an uploaded file to determine its true MIME type and description, and then compares these against a configured whitelist of allowed types for specific file extensions and endpoints. If the detected content does not match the expected signature, the upload is rejected early in the stream.

## GHSA-xgjr-7j9q-2h4r · github.com/knadh/listmonk

2026-11-06 · Go · **MODERATE** 5.5 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→SQL`

### Root cause

The `validateQueryTables` function, intended to prevent SQL injection by checking `EXPLAIN (FORMAT JSON)` output, was not passing all user-controlled query parameters to the `EXPLAIN` statement. This allowed an attacker to craft a malicious SQL query that would bypass the table access control check, as the `EXPLAIN` plan would not accurately reflect the actual query execution with the injected parameters.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary SQL queries, potentially leaking sensitive information such as admin password hashes and SMTP credentials from tables not explicitly allowed by the `allowedSubQueryTables` whitelist.

### Key diff

```
--- a/internal/core/subscribers.go
+++ b/internal/core/subscribers.go
@@ -593,7 +601,7 @@ func (c *Core) getSubscriberCount(searchStr, queryExp, subStatus string, listIDs
 }
 
 // validateQueryTables checks if the query accesses only allowed tables.
-func validateQueryTables(db *sqlx.DB, query string, allowedTables map[string]struct{}) error {
+func validateQueryTables(db *sqlx.DB, query string, allowedTables map[string]struct{}, args ...any) error {
 	// Get the EXPLAIN (FORMAT JSON) output.
 	tx, err := db.BeginTxx(context.Background(), &sql.TxOptions{ReadOnly: true})
 	if err != nil {
@@ -602,7 +610,7 @@ func validateQueryTables(db *sqlx.DB, query string, allowedTables map[string]struc
 	defer tx.Rollback()
 
 	var plan string
-	if err = tx.QueryRow("EXPLAIN (FORMAT JSON) "+query, nil, models.SubscriberStatusEnabled, "", 0, 10).Scan(&plan); err != nil {
+	if err = tx.QueryRow("EXPLAIN (FORMAT JSON) "+query, args...).Scan(&plan); err != nil {
 		return err
 	}
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `validateQueryTables` function to accept and pass all relevant query arguments to the `EXPLAIN (FORMAT JSON)` statement. This ensures that the `EXPLAIN` plan accurately reflects the full query, including user-controlled parameters, thereby correctly validating table access and preventing SQL injection.

## GHSA-3gq2-x4qg-p4g6 · github.com/canonical/lxd

2026-11-06 · Go · **MODERATE** 5.0 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from the `ImageDownload` function's handling of image sources. Previously, it directly used `args.Server` to connect to remote image servers without proper validation or restriction, allowing an authenticated user to specify an arbitrary internal or external URL. This enabled the LXD daemon to make requests to internal network resources.

### Impact

An authenticated attacker could leverage this vulnerability to probe the internal network of the LXD host, potentially discovering services or sensitive information that would otherwise be inaccessible. This could be a stepping stone for further attacks.

### Key diff

```
-	remote, err = lxd.ConnectPublicLXD(args.Server, clientArgs)
+		// Connect to the remote image server.
+		remote, err = registry.ConnectImageRegistry(ctx, s, *imageRegistry)
```

### Fix summary

The patch refactors the image download logic to introduce an `ImageRegistry` concept. Instead of directly using an arbitrary `args.Server` URL, images are now fetched via pre-configured and validated image registries. This ensures that only allowed and trusted endpoints can be accessed for image downloads, preventing arbitrary URL access.

## GHSA-92r3-p8c2-3fpx · org.eclipse.hawkbit:hawkbit-ddi-resource

2026-11-06 · Java · **MODERATE** 4.3 · Pattern: `PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION→ROLE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an insufficient authorization check in the DDI (Device Data Interface) component. Specifically, the query language (QL) builder allowed for the construction of queries that could bypass tenant isolation, enabling users to access firmware information belonging to other tenants.

### Impact

An attacker could exfiltrate tenant-isolated firmware information, gaining unauthorized access to sensitive data that should be restricted to specific tenants. This constitutes a privilege escalation, as a user could access data beyond their intended scope.

### Key diff

```
--- a/hawkbit-ql-jpa/src/main/java/org/eclipse/hawkbit/ql/jpa/SpecificationBuilder.java
+++ b/hawkbit-ql-jpa/src/main/java/org/eclipse/hawkbit/ql/jpa/SpecificationBuilder.java
@@ -152,10 +151,9 @@ private Predicate predicate(final Comparison comparison) {
                                 String.format("Operator %s is not supported for map fields with value null", op));
                     };
                 } else {
-                    final MapJoin<?, ?, ?> mapPath = (MapJoin<?, ?, ?>) pathResolver.getPath(attribute);
-                    return isNot(op)
-                            ? compare(comparison, toMapValuePath(pathResolver.getJoinOnInner(attribute, split[1])))
-                            : cb.and(equal(mapPath.key(), split[1]), compare(comparison, toMapValuePath(mapPath)));
+                    // map entry with key not null (exist) - use left join per key, key/value filtered in where
+                    final MapJoin<?, ?, ?> mapJoin = pathResolver.getJoinForWhere(attribute, split[1]);
+                    return cb.and(equal(mapJoin.key(), split[1]), compare(comparison, toMapValuePath(mapJoin)));
                 }
             } else if (attribute instanceof SetAttribute<?, ?> setAttribute) {
                 if (split.length < 2 || ObjectUtils.isEmpty(split[1])) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `SpecificationBuilder` to correctly handle map fields and one-to-many relationships in queries, ensuring that tenant isolation is properly enforced. It also refines the handling of LIKE/NOT LIKE operations on non-string fields to prevent unexpected behavior and potential information leakage.
